L ## Usage
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown
T 8 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L ### Welcome to cJSON.
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L cJSON aims to be the dumbest possible parser that you can get your job done with.
T 81 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L It's a single file of C, and a single header file.
T 50 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L JSON is described best here: http://www.json.org/
T 49 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L It's like XML, but fat-free. You use it to move data around, store things, or just
T 82 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L generally represent your program's state.
T 41 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L As a library, cJSON exists to take away as much legwork as it can, but not get in your way.
T 91 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L As a point of pragmatism (i.e. ignoring the truth), I'm going to say that you can use it
T 88 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L in one of two modes: Auto and Manual. Let's have a quick run-through.
T 69 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L I lifted some JSON from this page: http://www.json.org/fatfree.html
T 67 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L That page inspired me to write cJSON, which is a parser that tries to share the same
T 84 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L philosophy as JSON itself. Simple, dumb, out of the way.
T 56 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L ### Building
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown
T 12 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L There are several ways to incorporate cJSON into your project.
T 62 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### copying the source
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 23 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L Because the entire library is only one C file and one header file, you can just copy `cJSON.h` and `cJSON.c` to your projects source and start using it.
T 85 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 86 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 93 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 94 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 99 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 100 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 107 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 108 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 152 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L cJSON is written in ANSI C (C89) in order to support as many platforms and compilers as possible.
T 97 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### CMake
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 10 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L With CMake, cJSON supports a full blown build system. This way you get the most features. CMake with an equal or higher version than 3.5 is supported. With CMake it is recommended to do an out of tree build, meaning the compiled files are put in a directory separate from the source files. So in order to build cJSON with CMake on a Unix platform, make a `build` directory and run CMake inside it.
T 355 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 356 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 361 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 362 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 397 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown
L mkdir build
T 11 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L cd build
T 8 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L cmake ..
T 8 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown
L 
L This will create a Makefile and a bunch of other files. You can then compile it:
T 80 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown
L make
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown
L 
L And install it with `make install` if you want. By default it installs the headers `/usr/local/include/cjson` and the libraries to `/usr/local/lib`. It also installs files for pkg-config to make it easier to detect and use an existing installation of CMake. And it installs CMake config files, that can be used by other CMake based projects to discover the library.
T 20 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown

L 
L If you want, you can install the compiled library to your system using `make install`. By default it will install the headers in `/usr/local/include/cjson` and the libraries in `/usr/local/lib`. But you can change this behavior by setting the `PREFIX` and `DESTDIR` variables: `make PREFIX=/usr DESTDIR=temp install`. And uninstall them with: `make PREFIX=/usr DESTDIR=temp uninstall`.

L An item of this type represents a JSON value. The type is stored in `type` as a bit-flag (**this means that you cannot find out the type by just comparing the value of `type`**).

Also note that cJSON doesn't check if it is valid JSON.

L 
L If you have a rough idea of how big your resulting string will be, you can use `cJSON_PrintBuffered(const cJSON *item, int prebuffer, cJSON_bool fmt)`. `fmt` is a boolean to turn formatting with whitespace on and off. `prebuffer` specifies the first buffer size to use for printing. `cJSON_Print` currently uses 256 bytes for its first buffer size. Once printing runs out of space, a new buffer is allocated and the old gets copied over before printing is continued.

T 466 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L These dynamic buffer allocations can be completely avoided by using `cJSON_PrintPreallocated(cJSON *item, char *buffer, const int length, const cJSON_bool format)`. It takes a buffer to a pointer to print to and its length. If the length is reached, printing will fail and it returns `0`. In case of success, `1` is returned. Note that you should provide 5 bytes more than is actually needed, because cJSON is not 100% accurate in estimating if the provided memory is enough.

L ### Caveats
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown
T 11 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L #### Zero Character
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 19 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L cJSON doesn't support strings that contain the zero character `'\0'` or `\u0000`. This is impossible with the current API because strings are zero terminated.
T 62 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 63 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 67 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 68 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 72 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 73 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 79 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 80 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 158 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### Character Encoding
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 23 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L cJSON only supports UTF-8 encoded input. In most cases it doesn't reject invalid UTF-8 as input though, it just propagates it through as is. As long as the input doesn't contain invalid UTF-8, the output will always be valid UTF-8.
T 231 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### C Standard
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 15 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L cJSON is written in ANSI C (or C89, C90). If your compiler or C library doesn't follow this standard, correct behavior is not guaranteed.
T 137 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L NOTE: ANSI C is not C++ therefore it shouldn't be compiled with a C++ compiler. You can compile it with a C compiler and link it with your C++ code however. Although compiling with a C++ compiler might work, correct behavior is not guaranteed.
T 243 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### Floating Point Numbers
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 27 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L cJSON does not officially support any `double` implementations other than IEEE754 double precision floating point numbers. It might still work with other implementations but bugs with these will be considered invalid.
T 38 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 39 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 45 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 46 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 217 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L The maximum length of a floating point literal that cJSON supports is currently 63 characters.
T 94 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### Deep Nesting Of Arrays And Objects
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 39 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L cJSON doesn't support arrays and objects that are nested too deeply because this would result in a stack overflow. To prevent this cJSON limits the depth to `CJSON_NESTING_LIMIT` which is 1000 by default but can be changed at compile time.
T 157 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 158 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 177 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 178 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 239 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### Thread Safety
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 18 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L In general cJSON is **not thread safe**.
T 20 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 22 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.bold.markdown punctuation.definition.bold.markdown
T 37 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.bold.markdown
T 39 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.bold.markdown punctuation.definition.bold.markdown
T 40 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L However it is thread safe under the following conditions:
T 57 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L * `cJSON_GetErrorPtr` is never used (the `return_parse_end` parameter of `cJSON_ParseWithOpts` can be used instead)
T 1 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown punctuation.definition.list.begin.markdown
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown

T 41 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 42 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 58 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 59 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 73 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 74 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 93 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 94 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 115 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L * `cJSON_InitHooks` is only ever called before using cJSON in any threads.
T 1 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown punctuation.definition.list.begin.markdown
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 18 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 19 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 74 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L * `setlocale` is never called before all calls to cJSON functions have returned.
T 1 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown punctuation.definition.list.begin.markdown
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 12 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown

T 80 text.html.markdown markup.list.unnumbered.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### Case Sensitivity
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L When cJSON was originally created, it didn't follow the JSON standard and didn't make a distinction between uppercase and lowercase letters. If you want the correct, standard compliant, behavior, you need to use the `CaseSensitive` functions where available.
T 216 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 217 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 230 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 231 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 258 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L #### Duplicate Object Members
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown
T 29 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.4.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L cJSON supports parsing and printing JSON that contains objects that have multiple members with the same name. `cJSON_GetObjectItemCaseSensitive` however will always only return the first one.
T 110 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 111 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
